			// Apply the local archive-bar preference before shared HTML can paint.
			try {
				document.documentElement.dataset.archiveBarHidden = String(
					localStorage.getItem('platform-2026-archive-bar-hidden') === '1'
				);

		<script nonce="nJemiHVvkCXDAnBf1qclzw==">
			// Pre-paint theme: mirror mode-watcher's setInitialMode exactly
			// (modeStorageKey "platform-2026-theme", darkClassNames ["dark"],
			// default "system") so the stored mode is applied before first paint —
			// dark users never see a light flash. Runtime toggling stays with
			// <ModeWatcher>; this only seeds the initial class so they agree.
			(function () {
				try {
					var el = document.documentElement;
					var mode = localStorage.getItem('platform-2026-theme') || 'system';
					var light =
						mode === 'light' ||
						(mode === 'system' && window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: light)').matches);
					el.classList.toggle('dark', !light);
					el.style.colorScheme = light ? 'light' : 'dark';
				} catch (e) {}
			})();
		</script>
		<!-- Pre-paint chrome state (Phase 2/3). Fresh `chrome_hint` ⇒ signed-in
		     chrome dataset; else `viewer_present` marker ⇒ `viewerPending` NEUTRAL
		     chrome (skeleton avatar, no "Sign in" affordance) until the client
		     reconciles — killing the anonymous→signed-in flash on edge-cached
		     anonymous HTML. Structural check only; the HMAC server signature stays
		     the sole authority and the marker is never authority. Fails silent.
		     SYNC: this is `PREPAINT_CHROME_SNIPPET` from
		     `scripts/prepaint-chrome-snippet.ts` inlined verbatim (a pre-paint inline
		     script cannot import across the scripts/ boundary); the guard test
		     `prepaint-chrome-snippet.test.ts` fails if this drifts. -->
		<script nonce="nJemiHVvkCXDAnBf1qclzw==">
			(function () {
				try {
					var el = document.documentElement;
					var m = document.cookie.match(/(?:^|;\s*)chrome_hint=([^;]+)/);
					if (m) {
						var raw = decodeURIComponent(m[1]);
						var dot = raw.lastIndexOf('.');
						if (dot > 0) {
							var b64 = raw.slice(0, dot).replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/');
							var payload = JSON.parse(atob(b64));
							var auds = { free: 1, subscribed: 1, anonymous: 1 };
							if (payload && payload.v === 1 && auds[payload.aud]) {
								var age = Date.now() - payload.iat;
								if (age >= 0 && age < 5 * 60 * 1000) {
									el.dataset.authenticated = 'true';
									el.dataset.audience = payload.aud;
									return;
								}
							}
						}
					}
					if (/(?:^|;\s*)viewer_present=/.test(document.cookie)) {
						el.dataset.viewerPending = 'true';
					}
				} catch (e) {}
			})();
		</script>
		<!-- Typekit, non-render-blocking (db-speed-B 4.3): preconnect + preload, then

		     name system fallbacks ('Helvetica Neue', Georgia, …) → text paints in a
		     fallback immediately and swaps when the kit resolves (FOUT, never FOIT). -->

			// CSP forbids inline event handlers (script-src-attr 'none'), so the
			// classic onload="this.media='all'" swap moves here: still non-blocking —
			// media="print" defers apply, this flips it once the sheet arrives.
			(function () {
				try {
					var l = document.getElementById('typekit-css');
					if (!l) return;
					var swap = function () {
						l.media = 'all';
					};
					if (l.sheet) swap();
					else l.addEventListener('load', swap, { once: true });
				} catch (e) {}
			})();
		</script>

Pir","@type":"Person"},"headline":"pr calls","isPartOf":{"url":"https://sacred-texts.com/book/an-essay-on-the-pronunciation-of-enochian","name":"An Essay on the Pronunciation of Enochian","@type":"Book","author":{"name":"Christeos Pir","@type":"Person"},"inLanguage":"en"},"inLanguage":"en","description":"AN ESSAY ON ENOCHIAN PRONUNCIATION by Christeos Pir Fr. VITRIOL 335 0o O.T.O. Do what thou wilt shall be the whole of the Law. This paper is divided into two sections: a comparison of some systems of pronunciation of the Enochian or Angelic language as used in the nineteen Calls of the tablets, and an attempt to write the Calls in such a manner that they may be pronounced according to Dr. Dee's notes. Part One: The first section compares three versions of the Calls: that of the Aurum Solis as published in the Llewellyn edition of Denning and Phillips' \"Mysteria Magica\"; Geoffrey James' \"corrected\" calls as published in the Heptangle edition of \"The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee\"; and Dr. Dee's original notes as published in the Magickal Childe edition of Meric Causabon's \"True and Faithful Relation...\". I have compared these systems word-by-word, with the Aurum Solis and James versions on the first two lines, and Dee's on the third. Example: Aurum Solis: OL^ SONuF VORoSiG, GOHO I-AD BALaT, Geoffrey James: OL SONF VORSG, GOHO IAD BALT, Dee: Ol sonf vorsg, goho yad balt, Where Dee has separated a word into its syllables (eg, Im ua mar), I have hyphenated it so as to more easily discern between word divisions and syllable divisions (Im-ua-mar). Where he has shown stress by using an accent, I have used boldface (q-a-an). In a number of places his marginalia give more than one version of a word, usually in order to clarify its pronunciation; other times he has occasionally written an example for the same purpose -- these I have placed in footnotes. There are also one or two places where he has offered variant spellings: these, too, I have placed in the footnotes. I have attempted to standardize the punctuation, arbitrarily picking one of several possible systems and applying it to all of them in a manner somewhat reminiscent of Procrustes. One problem that had to be dealt with in re-typing the Aurum Solis version is their system of transliteration, which uses several symbols outside of the English alphabet. I have made two changes to their system, solely in order to use symbols that my computer could produce, as follows (the revised key, which is adapted from \"Mysteria Magica\", and applies only to the AS version, is given in the Appendix): ^ represents the neutral vowel, as the first vowel sound in \"parade,\" or the second vowel sound in \"column.\" # represents the guttural, pronounced as the \"ch\" in German (Woche, suchen). It is to be noted that James' version contains a number of differences from Dee's originals. These, as I understand it, are the result of his research into, and attempts to rectify, variations among the Calls themselves, and between the Enochian of the Calls and their English translations. I have not done any research in this area, and leave it up to the reader to decide which version to follow. Some of those corrections which are meant to fix transcription errors on Dee's part directly contradict some of Dee's own pronunciation","mainEntityOfPage":"https://sacred-texts.com/book/an-essay-on-the-pronunciation-of-enochian/pr-calls"}],"@context":"https://schema.org"}</script><!----><!--]--> <meta property="og:site_name" content="Internet Sacred Text Archive"/> <meta property="og:type" content="article"/> <meta property="og:title" content="pr calls – An Essay on the Pronunciation of Enochian"/> <meta property="og:description" content="Christeos Pir compares three pronunciation systems for the Enochian Calls and proposes a transcription based on Dr. Dee's notes and marginalia."/> <meta property="og:url" content="https://sacred-texts.com/pag/pr_calls.txt"/> <meta property="og:image" content="https://sacred-texts.com/og-logo.png"/> <!--[-1--><!--]--> <!--[-1--><!--]--> <!--[-1--><!--]--> <!--[-1--><!--]--> <meta name="twitter:card" content="summary"/> <meta name="twitter:title" content="pr calls – An Essay on the Pronunciation of Enochian"/> <meta name="twitter:description" content="Christeos Pir compares three pronunciation systems for the Enochian Calls and proposes a transcription based on Dr. Dee's notes and marginalia."/> <!--[-1--><!--]--> <meta name="twitter:image" content="https://sacred-texts.com/og-logo.png"/> <!--[-1--><!--]--> <!--[0--><meta name="robots" content="index,follow"/> <!--[--><!--]--><!--]--><!----><title>pr calls – An Essay on the Pronunciation of Enochian</title><style id="svelte-1hgbdq1">.mm-public-variant.svelte-1hgbdq1 {display:none;}

VITRIOL 335 0o O.T.O.</p><p>Do what thou wilt shall be the whole of the Law.</p><p>This paper is divided into two sections: a comparison of some systems of pronunciation of the Enochian or Angelic language as used in the nineteen Calls of the tablets, and an attempt to write the Calls in such a manner that they may be pronounced according to Dr. Dee's notes.</p><p>Part One:</p><p>The first section compares three versions of the Calls: that of the Aurum Solis as published in the Llewellyn edition of Denning and Phillips' "Mysteria Magica"; Geoffrey James' "corrected" calls as published in the Heptangle edition of "The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee"; and Dr. Dee's original notes as published in the Magickal Childe edition of Meric Causabon's "True and Faithful Relation...". I have compared these systems word-by-word, with the Aurum Solis and James versions on the first two lines, and Dee's on the third.</p><p>Example:</p><p>Aurum Solis: OL^ SONuF VORoSiG, GOHO I-AD BALaT, Geoffrey James: OL SONF VORSG, GOHO IAD BALT,</p><p>Dee: Ol sonf vorsg, goho yad balt,</p><p>Where Dee has separated a word into its syllables (eg, Im ua mar), I have hyphenated it so as to more easily discern between word divisions and syllable divisions (Im-ua-mar). Where he has shown stress by using an accent, I have used boldface (q-a-an). In a number of places his marginalia give more than one version of a word, usually in order to clarify its pronunciation; other times he has occasionally written an example for the same purpose -- these I have placed in footnotes. There are also one or two places where he has offered variant spellings: these, too, I have placed in the footnotes. I have attempted to standardize the punctuation, arbitrarily picking one of several possible systems and applying it to all of them in a manner somewhat reminiscent of Procrustes.</p><p>One problem that had to be dealt with in re-typing the Aurum Solis version is their system of transliteration, which uses several symbols outside of the English alphabet. I have made two changes to their system, solely in order to use symbols that my computer could produce, as follows (the revised key, which is adapted from "Mysteria Magica", and applies only to the AS version, is given in the Appendix): ^ represents the neutral vowel, as the first vowel sound in "parade," or the second vowel sound in "column." # represents the guttural, pronounced as the "ch" in German (Woche, suchen).</p><p>It is to be noted that James' version contains a number of differences from Dee's originals. These, as I understand it, are the result of his research into, and attempts to rectify, variations among the Calls themselves, and between the Enochian of the Calls and their English translations. I have not done any research in this area, and leave it up to the reader to decide which version to follow. Some of those corrections which are meant to fix transcription errors on Dee's part directly contradict some of Dee's own pronunciation notes. How Dee could have been able to record the sounds of his typos, I have not been able to determine.</p><p>I have chosen not to include the Golden Dawn's "Hebraicized" pronunciation, since it is based on a very different system. This is not meant as any reflection on the remarkable achievements of Westcott, Mathers and their descendants, which just go to prove the amazing versatility of the Enochian system.</p><p>Part Two:</p><p>The second section is my attempt to rewrite the Calls into some sort of standardized, readable phonetic system. One thing became evident in this process: the pronunciation guide given by Donald Laycock in his "Enochian Dictionary" is the closest thing to Dee's notes I've seen so far. For this reason, some may prefer to follow Laycock's guide in those cases where no indication in Dee's notes was forthcoming (eg: VLCININ = Vul-see-nin or Vul-kih-nin?), a not infrequent problem, unfortunately. I am not completely in agreement with Laycock in all instances, as may be seen by comparing my pronunciation key -see Part Two- with that in Laycock's "Enochian Dictionary," which I have printed in the Appendix. One such problem, and a bone of contention among scholars and dabblers alike, is over the use of the morpheme "zod." I regret to say that I have been unable to resolve this question. Out of the 127 instances of words with the letter "z" in the nineteen Calls, only 7 use "zod" -- and even then it is inconsistent: one of those words is "zod-a-car" which appears only once in that form, and 13 times as "zacar" (or "zacare"). It is not even clear whether it is intended as a spoken syllable, or merely a clarification:</p><p>The letter z has not always been called "zed" or "zee"; it has had many names, among them being "izzard", and, at the end of the sixteenth century, "ezod". "Zod" is nothing but a variant of this last name.</p><p>(Laycock, The Complete Enochian Dictionary, p. 45)</p><p>The angels themselves are no help here:</p><p>Dee: I pray you, is Mozod a word of three letters, or of five?</p><p>Nalvage: In wrote three, it is larger extended. [Dee- Z extended is Zod.]</p><p>Dee: Will you pardon me if I ask you another question of this extension?</p><p>Nal.: Say on: Moz in itself signifieth Joy; but Mozod extended, signifieth the Joy of God</p><p>(Causabon, A True and Faithful Relation..., p. 75)</p><p>Also:</p><p>"zod-lida ... It is a word and a letter."</p><p>(Causabon, op. cit., p. 120)</p><p>In the end, about all that can be said with any certainty about "zod" is that no one knows exactly how Dee intended it to be used. In fact, Geoffrey James doesn't even believe Enochian was received as a spoken language at all:</p><p>Unfortunately, Kelly never, as the spirits' "mouthpiece", pronounced the Angelical words.Kelly dictated letter by letter from a table that he saw in the crystal, as shown from Kelly's description that Dee recorded. . . Furthermore, if Angelical letter arrangement has random characteristics, as Laycock claims, then the English-like pronunciation cannot be an inherent quality of the language itself. Far more likely is that Dee assigned pronunciations to the Angelical because he wished to speak the keys in a ceremony, and, being English, adapted them as well as he could to his native tongue. Indeed, outside of few minor suggestions, the spirits seem unconcerned with pronunciation.</p><p>(James, The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee, Preface, p. xxiv)</p><p>A closer reading of Dee's original records, however, indicates that not only did Kelly hear the sounds spoken by the Angels while the letters were being shown, but that Dee re-checked the Calls with them several times, as a number of corrections and revisions were later recorded. Furthermore, if the pronunciation had been invented by Dee, surely he would have come up with something more consistent!</p><p>Dee's records show that the Calls were originally received in a different order than that in which they were later arranged by Dee, at the Angels' orders. For those interested in the original arrangement, I recommend Causabon, pps. 79 - 138, 190 -200.</p><p>One last note:</p><p>It has been pointed out by several people that one of the most striking things about the whole Enochian system is that How Much You Put Into It seems to matter a great deal more than How You Do It. Perhaps there's something to James' reasoning, after all.</p><p>I make no claims that this guide will make you any better (though hopefully no worse) of a magician. My intent was solely to attempt to compare the various systems of pronunciation with Dr. Dee's original notes on the subject. It is to him, and to my many mentors both within and without the Order, that this paper is dedicated with respect and love.</p><p>Love is the law, love under will.</p><p>Christeos Pir</p><p>Fr. VITRIOL 335, 0o O.T.O.</p><p>_O_ |</p><p>The First Call</p><p>OL^ SONuF VORoSiG, GOHO I-AD BALaT, LON^Si# KALaZ VO-NuPiHO: SOBRA OL SONF VORSG, GOHO IAD BALT, LANSH CALZ VONPHO: SOBRA Ol sonf vorsg, yad balt, lonsh calz vonpho: sobra</p><p>ZOL^ RO-R^ I TA NAZePiSAD GRA-A TA MALPiRoG: DeS HOLA# #A-A ZOL ROR I TA NAZPSAD OD GRAA TA MALPRG: DS HOLQ QAA zol ror I ta nazpsad graa ta malpurg: ds holqu-u q-a-a</p><p>NOT^HO-A ZIMuZ, OD KOM^MA# TA NOBLO# ZI-EN: SO-BA T^HIL G^NONuP NOTHOA ZIMZ, OD COMMAH TA NOBLOH ZIEN: SOBA THIL GNONP nothoa zimz, od commah ta nobloh zien: soba thil gnonp</p><p>PiRoGE AL^DI, DeS URoBeS OBO-LE# GRoSAM^. KASARoM OHO-RE-LA PRGE ALDI, DS URBS OBOLEH GRSAM. CASARM OHORELA purge aldi, ds urbs oboleh gursam. Casarm ohorela</p><p>KABA PIR^ DeS ZON^RENSiG KAB ERoM I-AD^NA#. PILA# FARoZeM CABA PIR DS ZONRENSG CAB ERM IADNAH. PILAH FARZM OD caba pir ds zonrensg cab erm Yadnah. P-il-ah farzm</p><p>ZUR^SA AD^NA GONO I-AD^PIL DeS HOM^ TO#, SO-BA ZNRZA ADNA OD GONO IADPIL DS HOM OD TOH, SOBA IAOD znurza adna (ds) gono iadpil ds hom toh, soba</p><p>IPAM, LU IPAMIS, DeS LO-HO-LO VEP^ ZOMuD</p><p>IPAM, OD UL IPAMIS, DS LOHOLO VEP ZOMD ipam, lu ipamis, ds loholo vep zomdv</p><p>PO-AMAL OD BOG^PA A-A-I TA PI-AP^ PI-AMOL OD VO-O-AN. POAMAL OD SONF AAI TA PIAP BALTOH OD VAOAN. poamal (s)od bogpa aai ta piap piama el od vaoan.</p><p>ZAKAR^, KA OD ZAM^RAN; ODO KIKLE #A-A; ZOR^GE, LAP^ ZACAR, CA, OD ZAMRAN; ODO CICLE QAA; ZORGE, LAP Zacar(e) c-a, od zamran; odo cicle qaa; zorge, lap</p><p>ZI-R^DO NOKO MAD, HO-ATa# I-A-IDA. ZIRDO NOCO MAD, HOATH IAIDA. zirdo noco mad, hoath Jaida.</p><p>Second Call</p><p>AD^GaT V^PA-A# ZON^GOM FA-A-IP^ SALD^, VI-IV aL^, SO-BAM ADGT UPAAH ZONG OM FAAIP SALD, VIV L, SOBAM Adgt V-pa-ah zong om fa-a-ip sald, vi-iv L, s-o-bam</p><p>I-ALPiRoG IZA-ZAZ PI-AD^PI#; KA-SAR-MA AB^RAM^G^ TA TAL^HO IALPRG IZAZAZ PIADPH; CASARMA ABRAMG TA TALHO i-al-purg izazaz piadph; casarma abramg ta talho</p><p>PA-RA-KLEDA, #aTA LORoSiLa# TURoBeS O-OGE BAL^TO#. GI-VI K^HIS PARACLEDA, Q TA LORSLQ TURBS OOGE BALTOH. GIUI CHIS paracleda, quu-ta lors-l-qua turbs ooge baltoh. Giui chis</p><p>LU-SiD OR^RI, OD MIKALaP K^HIS BI-A O-ZON^GON, LAP^ NO-AN TaROF^ LUSD ORRI, OD MICALP CHIS BIA OZONGON, LAP NOAN TROF lusd orri, od mi-calp chis bia ozongon, lap no-an trof</p><p>KO-RoS TA-GE O#^ MA-NIN I-A-I-DON. TOR^ZU GOHEL^: ZAKAR^ KA CORS TA GE OQ MANIN IAIDON. TORZU GOHEL: ZACAR CA cors tage o-qua manin ia-i-don. Torzu go-hel: zacar ca</p><p>K^NO#-OD, ZAM^RAN MIKAL^ZO OD OZAZeM U-RE-LaP, LAP^ ZI-R^ I-O-I-AD. ODO KIKLE #A-A; OD OZAZeMA SIBSI BUTMONA, DS SURZAS TIA BALTAN. ODO CICLE QAA; OD OZAZMA sib-si but-mo-na, ds sur-zas tia baltan. Odo cicle qaa; od ozazma</p><p>PiLA-PiLI I-AD-NA-MAD^. PLAPLI IADNAMAD. pla-pli Iad-na-mad.</p><p>PART TWO</p><p>As noted before, there is a lot of room for debate in those areas where Dee's notes are inconclusive or contradictory. This guide is my own interpretation (and occasionally, I admit, extrapolation) of Dee's pronunciation. Another, that of Donald Laycock, is in the Appendix to this paper. It is my opinion that often the differences between these variations are more of emphasis rather than any fundamental discord: between "ee" and "ih" for instance.</p><p>In the version of the Calls that follows, the first line is that of the Calls as printed in Causabon (except where noted), the second and third are my phonetic version with possible variations.</p><p>PRONUNCIATION KEY</p><p>A - as in "father", except "ag" rhymes with "nag" C - as k before a, o, u (some exceptions) - as s before i, e (some exceptions) E - stressed: as in "obey" - unstressed: as in "beg" (some exceptions) G - as (hard) g before a, o, u - usually as j before i, e, and word-final, occasionally in other places, some exceptions H - usually as h, except in combinations (ch, ph, sh, th); either silent or slightly aspirated in certain locations (eg:</p><p>Comselh). This last does not apply to the phonetic notation that follows: duh-loo-gah (dluga) does not end with an aspi- rated sound. I - stressed: as in "machine" - unstressed: as in "pill" I,J - as y or jh ("ge" as in "garage") in initial position and before a vowel O - stressed: as in "go" - unstressed: as in "go" or as in "pot", especially in -och-</p><p>(rhymes with "botch") Q,QU - as "kuh", or "kwuh" (as in "quick") U - usually as in "rule", but occasionally as in "mug" - in initial position may be pronounced as "yew" X - as in "fox" Y - usually as under key for I and I,J Z - as in "zoo"; in a very few words, as zod CH - as k in most positions, but -"och" rhymes with "botch" PH - as f SH - as sh TH - as th</p><p>Double vowels should be pronounced only partly separately, with an elided pause between them: QAA becomes Kuh-ah-ah, with only the barest hesitation. The First Call</p><p>Ol sonf vorsg, goho iad balt, lansh calz vonpho: sobra zol ror I Ol sonf vors-juh, goho yad balt, lonsh calz vonfo: sobra zol ror ee vors-guh zod-ol</p><p>ta nazpsad graa ta malprg: ds holq qaa ta naz-puh-sad grah-ah ta malpurj: des hol-kwuh-uh kuh-ah-ah hol-kuh-uh kwuh-ah-ah</p><p>nothoa zimz, od commah ta nobloh zien: soba thil gnonp no-tho-ah zeemz, od com-mah ta no-blo zee-en: soba theel guh-non-puh zimz thil guh-nomp</p><p>prge aldi, ds urbs oboleh grsam. Casarm ohorela caba pir purj aldee, des yoorbs o-bo-lay gursam. Kasarm oho-ray-la kaba peer</p><p>ds zonrensg cab erm iadnah. Pilah farzm znrza adna des zon-rens-juh kab erm yad-nah. Niiso, bagle auauago gohon; niiso bagle kar-baf? Nee-ee-so, ba-glay ah-wah-wah-go go-hon; nee-ee-so ba-glay ah-va-va-go</p><p>momao siaion od mabza iadoiasmomar, poilp. Niis mo-ma-oh see-ah-ee-on od mab-za yad-oh-ee-as-mo-mar, po-eelp. Nih-ees po-ilp Nih-is</p><p>zamran ciaofi caosgo od bliors od corsi ta abramig. zam-ran kee-ah-oh-fee ka-os-go od blee-ors od kor-see ta a-bra-mig. see-ah-oh-fee</p><p>Ninth Call</p><p>Micaoli bransg purgel napta ialpor dsbrin Mee-ka-oh-lee brans-guh poor-gel nap-ta yal-por des-breen Mih-ka-oh-lee brans-juh poor-jel des-brin</p><div data-ad-vro="1" class="not-prose"><div class="ista-vro-reserve"></div></div><p>efafafe p vonpho olani od obza; sobca vpaah chis ay-fah-fah-fay puh von-fo oh-la-nee od ob-za; sob-ka vuh-pah-ah kis pay</p><p>tatan od tranan balye alar lusda soboln od chisholq ta-tan od tra-nan ba-lee-ay a-lar loos-da so-boln od kis-hol-kwah</p><p>cnoquodi cial. Vnal aldon mom caosgo ta lasollor kuh-no-kwo-dee see-al. Vuh-nal al-don mom ka-os-go ta las-ol-lor</p><p>gnay limlal; amma chiis sobca madrid zchis; ooanoan guh-nay lil-lal; am-ma kee-ees sob-ka ma-drid zod-kis; oh-oh-ah-no-an guh-nah-ee kih-ees</p><p>chis aviny drilpi caosgin od butmoni parm zumvi kis ah-vee-nee dril-pee ka-os-jin od boot-mo-nee parm zoom-vee ah-vih-nee ka-os-gin</p><p>cnila; dazis ethamz achildao, od mirk ozol chis kuh-nee-la; daz-ees ay-tham-zod ah-kil-da-oh, od mirk oh-zol kis</p><p>pidiai collal ulci-nin asobam ucim. Bagle? pee-dee-ah-ee kol-lal ool-see-nin a-so-bam oo-seem. Ba-glay? ool-see-neen oo-kim</p><p>Iadbaltoh chirlan par! Niiso od ip ofafafe, bagle Yad-bal-toh kir-lan par! Nee-ee-so od eep oh-fa-fa-fay, ba-glay</p><p>acocasb icorsca vnig blior. ah-ko-kasb ee-kors-ka vuh-nig blee-or.</p><p>Tenth Call</p><p>Coraxo chis cormp od blans lucal aziazor paeb, soba Ko-rak-so kis kormp od blanz loo-kal ah-zee-ah-zor pa-eb, so-ba</p><p>lilonon chis op virq eophan od raclir maasi bagle lee-lo-non kis op veer-kwuh ay-oh-fan od ra-kleer ma-ah-see ba-glay vir-kwuh</p><p>caosgi, ds ialpon dosig od basgim; od oxex dazis ka-os-jee, des yal-pon do-sig od bas-geem; od oks-ayks dah-zees ka-os-gee jhal-pon bas-gim okx-eks</p><p>siatris od salbrox cynxir faboan. Unalchis const see-ah-trees od sal-broks sinks-eer fa-bo-an. Yoon-al-kis konst seenks-eer</p><p>ds daox cocasg ol oanio yor vohim ol gizyax od des da-oks ko-kas-juh ol oh-ah-nee-oh yor vo-heem ol giz-ee-aks od vo-him jiz-ee-aks</p><p>eors cocasg plosi molni ds pageip larag om droln ay-orz ko-kas-juh plo-see mol-nee des pa-gay-eep la-rag om drol-nuh pa-jay-eep</p><p>matorb cocasb emna. L patralx yolci nomig ma-torb ko-kas-buh em-na. El-pa-tralks yol-see no-mig yol-kee</p><p>monons olora gnay angelard. Ohio, ohio, ohio, mo-nons oh-lo-ra guh-nay an-gay-lard. O-hee-o, o-hee-o, o-hee-o, an-jeh-lard</p><p>ohio, ohio, ohio, noib ohio caosgon! Bagle O-hee-o, o-hee-o, o-hee-o, no-eeb, o-hee-o ka-os-gon! Ba-glay</p><p>madrid i zirop chiso drilpa. Niiso: crip ip nidali. ma-drid ee zee-rop kee-so dril-pa. Nee-ee-so: kreep eep nee-da-lee. dreel-pa krip nih-da-lee</p><p>Eleventh Call</p><p>Oxiayal holdo od zirom o coraxo ds zildar Oks-ee-ah-al hol-do od zeer-om oh ko-raks-oh des zeel-dar</p><p>raasy; od vabzir camliax od bahal, niiso salman ra-ah-see; od vab-zeer kam-lee-aks od ba-hal, nee-ee-so sal-man</p><p>teloch casarman holq od ti ta zchis, soba cormf iga tay-lotch ka-sar-man hol-kwah od tee tah zod-kis, so-ba kormf ee-gah</p><p>niisa bagle abramg noncp. Zacar e ca, od zamran, odo nee-ee-sa ba-glay ah-bram-guh nonsp. Zakar ay ka, od zamran, odo</p><p>cicle qaa, zorge, lap zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. kee-klay kuh-ah-ah, zorj, lap zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah.</p><p>Jha-ee-dah</p><p>Twelfth Call</p><p>Nonci dsonf babage od chis ob hubaio tibibp, Non-see duh-son-fuh ba-ba-jeh od kis ob hoo-ba-ee-oh tee-beeb-puh, ba-bajay</p><p>allar atraah od ef. Drix fafen mian ar enay ovof, soba al-lar a-tra-ah od ef. Driks fa-fen mee-an ar en-ay oh-vof, so-ba</p><p>Dreeks ay-nah-ee</p><p>dooain aai ivonph. Zacar e ca, od zamran, odo cicle do-oh-ah-een ah-ah-ee ee-vonf. Zakar ay ka, od zamran, odo kee-klay</p><p>qaa, zorge, lap zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. kuh-ah-ah, zorj, lap zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah.</p><p>Jha-ee-dah.</p><p>Thirteenth Call</p><p>Napeai babagen ds brin ux ooana lring vonph Na-pay-ah-ee ba-ba-jen des breen yooks oh-oh-ah-na el-ring vonf el-rinj</p><p>doalim, eolis ollog orsba ds chis affa. Micma isro do-ah-leem ay-oh-lees ol-log orz-ba des kis af-fa. Mik-ma ees-ro do-ah-lim ay-oh-lis Meek-ma</p><p>mad od lonshitox ds jumbd. Zacar e ca, od zamran, odo mad od lon-shee-toks des juh-oomd. Zakar ay ka, od zamran, odo yoom-duh</p><p>cicle qaa, zorge, lap zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. kee-klay kuh-ah-ah, zorj, lap zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah.</p><p>Jha-ee-dah</p><p>Fourteenth Call</p><p>[No record of this call in Causabon. This version based on the Aurum Solis text.]</p><p>Noromi bagie pasbs oiad, ds trint mirc ol No-ro-mee ba-jee-ay pas-biz oh-ee-ad, des treen-tuh meer-kuh ol ba-gee-ay mirk</p><p>thil, dods tolham caosgo homin, ds brin oroch quar; theel, dods tol-ham ka-os-go ho-meen, des breen orok koo-ar; thil ho-min brin kwar</p><p>micma bial oiad, aisro tox dsium aai mik-ma bee-al oh-ee-ad, ah-ees-ro toks des-yoom ah-ah-ee meek-ma des-ee-oom</p><p>baltim. Zacar od zamran, odo cicle qaa, jorge, lap bal-teem. Zakar od zamran, odo kee-klay kuh-ah-ah, zorj, lap bal-tim kah-ah</p><p>zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah.</p><p>Jha-ee-dah</p><p>Fifteenth Call</p><p>[No record of this call in Causabon. This version based on the Aurum Solis text.]</p><p>Ils tabaan lialprt casarman upaahi chis darg ds Yils ta-ba-an lee-al-purt kasarman oo-pa-ah-hee kis darjuh des darguh</p><p>okido caosgi orscor; ds omax monasci baeouib od oh-kee-do ka-os-jee ors-kor; des oh-maks mo-nas-kee bye-oh-oo-eeb od ka-os-gee mo-nas-see bah-ay-oh-eeb</p><p>emetgis iaiadix. Zacar od zamran, odo cicle qaa ay-met-jis yah-ee-ah-diks. Zakar od zamran, odo kee-klay kuh-ah-ah eh-met-gis</p><p>zorge, lap zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. zorj, lap zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah.</p><p>Jha-ee-dah</p><p>Sixteenth Call</p><p>[Beginning of this call not recorded in Causabon. Missing text based on the Aurum Solis text.]</p><p>Ils viuialprt salman balt, ds brin acroodzi busd od Yils veev-yal-purt sal-man balt, des breen a-kroh-od-zee boosd od vee-oo-ee-al-purt</p><p>bliorax balit; dsinsi caosg lusdan emod dsom od blee-oh-raks baleet; des-in-see kaos-juh loos-dan ay-mod duh-som od bal-tuh koas-guh</p><p>tliob; drilpa geh yls Madzilodarp. Zacar e ca, od zamran, tuh-lee-ob; dreel-pa jeh yils mad-zee-lo-darp. Zakar ay ka, od zamran, dril-pa</p><p>odo cicle qaa, zorge, lap zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. odo kee-klay kuh-ah-ah, zorj, lap zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah. kah-ah Jha-ee-dah</p><p>Seventeenth Call</p><p>Ils dial pert soba vpaah chis nanba zixlay dodsih Yils dee-al pert so-ba vuh-pa-ah kis nan-ba zeeks-la-ee dod-seeh ziks-lay</p><p>od brint taxs Hubaro tastax ylsi, sobaiad od breent faksiz hoo-ba-ro tas-taks yil-see, so-ba-ee-ad brint so-ba-yad</p><p>ivonpounph, aldon daxil od toatar. Zacar e ca, od zamran, ee-von-po-oonf, al-don daks-il od to-a-tar. Zakar ay ka, od zamran,</p><p>odo cicle qaa, zorge, lap zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. odo kee-klay kuh-ah-ah, zorj, lap zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah. kah-ah Jha-ee-dah</p><p>Eighteenth Call</p><p>Ils micaolz olpirt yalpurg bliors ds odo busdir Yils mee-ka-ol-zod ol-peert yal-poorj blee-ors des odo boos-deer mih-ka-ol-zod buh-lee-rs</p><p>oiad ovoars caosgo casarmg laiad eran brints oh-ee-ad oh-vo-arz ka-os-go ka-sarm-juh la-ee-ad ay-ran breents eh-ran brints</p><p>cafafam, ds iumd aquulo adohi moz od maoffas bolp ka-fa-fam, des yoomd ah-kwuh-oo-lo a-do-hee moz od ma-of-as bolp ee-oomd</p><p>comobliort pambt. Zacar e ca, od zamran, odo cicle ko-mo-blee-ort pam-but. Zakar ay ka, od zamran, odo kee-klay pamt</p><p>qaa, zorge, lap zirdo noco mad, hoath Iaida. kuh-ah-ah, jorj, lap zeer-do noko mad, ho-ath Ya-ee-dah.</p><p>Jha-ee-dah</p><p>The Call of the Thirty Aethyrs</p><p>Madriax dspraf [LIL], chis micaolz saanir caosgo, Mad-ree-aks des praf [Leel], kis mee-ka-olz sa-ah-neer ka-os-go,</p><p>od fisis balzizras Iaida! Nonca gohulim, micma od fi-sees bal-zeez-ras Ya-ee-dah! Nonsa go-hoo-lim, meek-ma fee-seez Jha-ee-dah! mik-ma</p><p>adoian mad, Iaod bliorb, sabaooaona chis a-do-ee-an mad, Ee-ah-od blee-orb, sa-ba-oh-oh-ah-oh-na kis</p><p>Yah-od</p><p>luciftias peripsol, ds abraassa noncf netaaib loo-keef-tee-as pay-reep-sol, des a-bra-as-sa non-suf ne-tah-ah-eeb loo-seef-tee-as peh-reep-sol</p><p>caosgi od tilb adphaht damploz, tooat noncf ka-os-jee od teelb ad-fa-tuh dam-ploz, to-oh-at non-suf ka-os-gee</p><p>gmicalzoma l rasd tofglo marb yarry idoigo od guh-mee-kal-zo-ma el-ras-duh tof-glo marb yar-ree eed-wee-go od ee-doo-ee-go</p><p>torzulp iaodaf, gohol; caosga tabaord saanir tor-zoolp ya-oh-daf, go-hol; ka-os-ga ta-ba-ord sa-ah-neer</p><p>od christeos yrpoil tiobl, busdir tilb noaln od kris-tay-os eer-po-eel tee-oh-bul, boos-deer teel-buh no-al-nuh yur-po-eel til-buh</p><p>paid orsba od dodrmni zylna. Elzap tilb parmgi pa-eed ors-ba od dod-rum-nee zilna. El-zap-teelb parm-gee zeel-na El-zap-tilb parm-jee</p><p>peripsax od ta qurlst booapis. Lnibm ovcho symp, pay-reep-saks od ta koorlst bo-oh-ah-pees. El-nib-um oh-vuh-ko simp, pay-rip-saks</p><p>od christeos agtoltorn mirc q tiobl lel; ton paombd od kris-tay-os ag-tol-torn meerk kwah tee-oh-bel lel; ton pa-om-duh mirk</p><p>dilzmo aspian, od christeos agltertorn parach asymp; deelz-mo as-pee-an, od kris-tay-os ag-ul-ter-torn pa-rach a-simp;</p><p>cordziz dodpal od fifalz lsmnad, od fargt bams kord-zeez dod-pal od fee-falz ulz-muh-nad, od far-jet bamz far-gut</p><p>omaoas; conisbra od auavox tonug, orscatbl noasmi oh-ma-oh-as; ko-nees-bra od ah-wah-voks to-nuj, orz-cat-bul no-as-mee ko-nis-bra</p><p>tabges levithmong. Unchi omptilb ors. Bagle? Moooah tab-jess lay-veeth-mong. Oonki om-puh-teelb orz. Ba-glay? Mo-oh-oh-ah tab-gayz lay-vithmong om-puh-tilb</p><p>ol cordziz. L capimao ixomaxip od ca cocasb ol-cord-zeez. El-ka-pee-ma-oh eeks-oh-maks-eep od ka-ko-kas-buh</p><p>gosaa; baglen pii tianta ababalond, od faorgt go-sa-ah; ba-glen pee-ee tee-an-ta a-ba-ba-lon-duh, od fa-or-jet</p><p>telocvovim. Madriiax torzu; oadriax orocha tay-lotch-vo-veem. Ma-dree-yaks tor-zoo; oh-ah-dree-aks oh-ro-ka</p><p>aboapri. Tabaori priaz artabas; adrpan corsta ah-bo-ah-pree. Ta-ba-oh-ree pree-az ar-ta-bas; ah-dir-pan kor-sta</p><p>dobix. Yolcam priazi arcoazior, od quasb qting. do-biks. Yol-kam pree-ah-zee ar-ko-ah-zee-or, od kwas-buh kwah-tinj. kuh-tinj</p><p>Ripir paaoxt sagacor; vml od prdzar cacrg Ree-peer pa-ah-oks-tuh sa-ga-kor; vum-ul od purd-zar ka-kurj Ri-peer oom-ul ka-kurg</p><p>aoiveae cormpt. Torzu, zacar, od zamran aspt sibsi ah-oy-vay-ah-ay korm-put. Tor-zoo, zakar, od zamran as-put seeb-see ah-oh-ee-vay-ah-ay korm-puh-tuh sib-see</p><p>butmona, ds surzas tia baltan. Odo cicle qaa; od boot-mo-na, des soor-zas tee-ah bal-tan. Odo kee-klay kah-ah; od kuh-ah-ah ozazma plapli Iadnamad. oh-zaz-ma pla-plee Yad-na-mad.</p><p>Jhad-na-mad</p><p>THE NAMES OF THE THIRTY AETHYRS</p><p>LIL Leel ICH Eek ASP Ahs-puh ARN Arn LOE Lo-ay LIN Leen, Lin ZOM Zom ZIM Zeem, Zim TOR Tor PAZ Paz UTA Oo-tah NIA Nee-ah LIT Leet, Lit OXO Oh-kso UTI Oo-tee MAZ Maz LEA Lay-ah DES Dess DEO Day-oh TAN Tan ZAA Zah-ah ZID Zeed, Zid ZEN Zen BAG Bah-guh ZIP Zeep, Zip POP Poh-puh RII Ree-ee ZAX Zaks CHR Khar TEX Teks</p><p>APPENDIX</p><p>AURUM SOLIS PRONUNCIATION KEY</p><p>(from "Mysteria Magica" pg 416)</p><p>A as the first vowel sound in "father" E as the vowel sound in "say" I as the vowel sound in "meet" O as the vowel sound in "hold" U as the vowel sound in "food"</p><p>a as the vowel sound in "add" e as the vowel sound in "yet" i as the vowel sound in "sit" o as the vowel sound in "hot" u as the vowel sound in "foot"</p><p>^ represents the neutral vowel, as the first vowel sound in "parade," or the second vowel sound in "column."</p><p>Consonants are given their normal values (G invariably represents the hard sound of that letter, as in "gold."</p><p># represents the guttural, pronounced as the "ch" in German (Woche, suchen).</p><p>As a general rule, allowing for varied inflexions of the voice in the flow of magical utterance, all the syllables in an Enochian word should be given equal weight. It will be noted that hyphens are frequently inserted in words. These only emphasise the need to give full value to a vowel, or to a syllable, and do not call for a real break in the utterance.</p><p>DONALD LAYCOCK'S PRONUNCIATION KEY</p><p>(from "The Complete Enochian Dictionary" pps 46-47.)</p><p>A - long (stressed), as in "lah-di-dah" - short (unstressed), as in French "patte" B - usually as English b, but silent between m and another consonant, or after m finally C - as k before a, o, u (with some exceptions) - as s before i, e (with many exceptions), and in clusters of consonants (noncf = nonsf) CH - as k in most positions, but as ch finally D - as d in all positions E - when stressed, as in French "fee" - when unstressed, as in "bed" F - as f in all positions G - as (hard) g before a, o, u - as j before i, e, in final position, after d, and in clusters of consonants H - as h in most positions (except in combinations ch, ph, sh, th); silent after a vowel, but the vowel is lengthened I - when stressed, as in (French) "machine" - when unstressed, as in "bit" - in combinations: ai as in "fly"; ei as in "eight"; oi as in</p><p>"boil" - as y in word-initial position before a vowel (Iad = Yad) K - as k in all positions L - as l in all positions M - as m in all positions N - as n in all positions O - when stressed, as in French "mot" - when unstressed, as in "not" - in combinations: oi as in "boil"; ou as in "bout"; oo as in</p><p>"fool" P - as p except in combination ph PH - as f Q,QU - as kw (qu in "quick") -- but the word q is pronounced kwa R - as r in "right" (but may be rolled) S - usually as s in "sit" - sometimes z, in places where this is more natural in English</p><p>(lrasd = elrazd) SH - as sh in "ship" T - as t except in combination th TH - as th in "thank" U - as oo in "boot", or u in "put" - in initial position as "yew" - as v or w before another vowel, and in word-final position X - as x in "fox" Y - as y in word-initial position before a vowel - as the letter I before a consonant, and in word-final position Z - as z in "zoo"; in a very few words, as zod</p><p>BIBLIOGRAPHY</p><p>Causabon, Meric., D.D., A True and Faithful Relation of What Passed for Many Years Between Dr. John Dee and some Spirits, Magickal Childe, 1992, New York, NY.</p><p>Denning, Melita, and Phillips, Osborne, Mysteria Magica, Llewellyn Publications, 1992, St. Paul, MN.</p><p>James, Geoffrey, The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee, Heptangle Books, 1989, Gillette, NJ.</p><p>Laycock, Donald C., The Complete Enochian Dictionary, Askin Publishers, 1978, London, England, UK.</p><!----></div><!----><!----></div> <!--[-1--><!--]--></article></div><!--]--><!----><!----><!--]--></div><!----></article><!--]--><!--[2--><!--[0--><!--[-1--><article class="pg-placement Pronunciation of Enochian",bookSlug:"an-essay-on-the-pronunciation-of-enochian",bookAuthor:"Christeos Pir",bookDescription:"\u003Cp>This paper is divided into two sections: a comparison of some systems of pronunciation of the Enochian or Angelic language as used in the nineteen Calls of the tablets, and an attempt to write the Calls in such a manner that they may be pronounced according to Dr. Dee's notes.\u003C/p>",bookCoverUrl:null,bookUrl:"https://sacred-texts.com/pag/pr_calls.txt",yearPublished:null,publisherName:null,chapters:[],category:{categorySlug:"enochian-magic",categoryName:"Enochian Magic",sectionName:"Enochian Magic",categorySourceUrl:"https://sacred-texts.com/eso/enoch/index.htm"},contentMode:"database"},chapterContent:{id:309143,chapterId:309143,contentHtml:"\u003Cp>AN ESSAY ON ENOCHIAN PRONUNCIATION by\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Christeos Pir\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Fr. VITRIOL 335 0o O.T.O.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Do what thou wilt shall be the whole of the Law.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>This paper is divided into two sections: a comparison of some systems of pronunciation of the Enochian or Angelic language as used in the nineteen Calls of the tablets, and an attempt to write the Calls in such a manner that they may be pronounced according to Dr. Dee's notes.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Part One:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>The first section compares three versions of the Calls: that of the Aurum Solis as published in the Llewellyn edition of Denning and Phillips' \"Mysteria Magica\"; Geoffrey James' \"corrected\" calls as published in the Heptangle edition of \"The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee\"; and Dr. Dee's original notes as published in the Magickal Childe edition of Meric Causabon's \"True and Faithful Relation...\". I have compared these systems word-by-word, with the Aurum Solis and James versions on the first two lines, and Dee's on the third.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Example:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Aurum Solis: OL^ SONuF VORoSiG, GOHO I-AD BALaT, Geoffrey James: OL SONF VORSG, GOHO IAD BALT,\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Dee: Ol sonf vorsg, goho yad balt,\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Where Dee has separated a word into its syllables (eg, Im ua mar), I have hyphenated it so as to more easily discern between word divisions and syllable divisions (Im-ua-mar). Where he has shown stress by using an accent, I have used boldface (q-a-an). In a number of places his marginalia give more than one version of a word, usually in order to clarify its pronunciation; other times he has occasionally written an example for the same purpose -- these I have placed in footnotes. There are also one or two places where he has offered variant spellings: these, too, I have placed in the footnotes. I have attempted to standardize the punctuation, arbitrarily picking one of several possible systems and applying it to all of them in a manner somewhat reminiscent of Procrustes.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>One problem that had to be dealt with in re-typing the Aurum Solis version is their system of transliteration, which uses several symbols outside of the English alphabet. I have made two changes to their system, solely in order to use symbols that my computer could produce, as follows (the revised key, which is adapted from \"Mysteria Magica\", and applies only to the AS version, is given in the Appendix): ^ represents the neutral vowel, as the first vowel sound in \"parade,\" or the second vowel sound in \"column.\" # represents the guttural, pronounced as the \"ch\" in German (Woche, suchen).\u003C/p>\u003Cp>It is to be noted that James' version contains a number of differences from Dee's originals. These, as I understand it, are the result of his research into, and attempts to rectify, variations among the Calls themselves, and between the Enochian of the Calls and their English translations. I have not done any research in this area, and leave it up to the reader to decide which version to follow. Some of those corrections which are meant to fix transcription errors on Dee's part directly contradict some of Dee's own pronunciation notes. How Dee could have been able to record the sounds of his typos, I have not been able to determine.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>I have chosen not to include the Golden Dawn's \"Hebraicized\" pronunciation, since it is based on a very different system. This is not meant as any reflection on the remarkable achievements of Westcott, Mathers and their descendants, which just go to prove the amazing versatility of the Enochian system.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Part Two:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>The second section is my attempt to rewrite the Calls into some sort of standardized, readable phonetic system. One thing became evident in this process: the pronunciation guide given by Donald Laycock in his \"Enochian Dictionary\" is the closest thing to Dee's notes I've seen so far. For this reason, some may prefer to follow Laycock's guide in those cases where no indication in Dee's notes was forthcoming (eg: VLCININ = Vul-see-nin or Vul-kih-nin?), a not infrequent problem, unfortunately. I am not completely in agreement with Laycock in all instances, as may be seen by comparing my pronunciation key -see Part Two- with that in Laycock's \"Enochian Dictionary,\" which I have printed in the Appendix. One such problem, and a bone of contention among scholars and dabblers alike, is over the use of the morpheme \"zod.\" I regret to say that I have been unable to resolve this question. Out of the 127 instances of words with the letter \"z\" in the nineteen Calls, only 7 use \"zod\" -- and even then it is inconsistent: one of those words is \"zod-a-car\" which appears only once in that form, and 13 times as \"zacar\" (or \"zacare\"). It is not even clear whether it is intended as a spoken syllable, or merely a clarification:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>The letter z has not always been called \"zed\" or \"zee\"; it has had many names, among them being \"izzard\", and, at the end of the sixteenth century, \"ezod\". \"Zod\" is nothing but a variant of this last name.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>(Laycock, The Complete Enochian Dictionary, p. 45)\u003C/p>\u003Cp>The angels themselves are no help here:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Dee: I pray you, is Mozod a word of three letters, or of five?\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Nalvage: In wrote three, it is larger extended. [Dee- Z extended is Zod.]\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Dee: Will you pardon me if I ask you another question of this extension?\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Nal.: Say on: Moz in itself signifieth Joy; but Mozod extended, signifieth the Joy of God\u003C/p>\u003Cp>(Causabon, A True and Faithful Relation..., p. 75)\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Also:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>\"zod-lida ... It is a word and a letter.\"\u003C/p>\u003Cp>(Causabon, op. cit., p. 120)\u003C/p>\u003Cp>In the end, about all that can be said with any certainty about \"zod\" is that no one knows exactly how Dee intended it to be used. In fact, Geoffrey James doesn't even believe Enochian was received as a spoken language at all:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Unfortunately, Kelly never, as the spirits' \"mouthpiece\", pronounced the Angelical words.Kelly dictated letter by letter from a table that he saw in the crystal, as shown from Kelly's description that Dee recorded. . . Furthermore, if Angelical letter arrangement has random characteristics, as Laycock claims, then the English-like pronunciation cannot be an inherent quality of the language itself. Far more likely is that Dee assigned pronunciations to the Angelical because he wished to speak the keys in a ceremony, and, being English, adapted them as well as he could to his native tongue. Indeed, outside of few minor suggestions, the spirits seem unconcerned with pronunciation.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>(James, The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee, Preface, p. xxiv)\u003C/p>\u003Cp>A closer reading of Dee's original records, however, indicates that not only did Kelly hear the sounds spoken by the Angels while the letters were being shown, but that Dee re-checked the Calls with them several times, as a number of corrections and revisions were later recorded. Furthermore, if the pronunciation had been invented by Dee, surely he would have come up with something more consistent!\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Dee's records show that the Calls were originally received in a different order than that in which they were later arranged by Dee, at the Angels' orders. For those interested in the original arrangement, I recommend Causabon, pps. 79 - 138, 190 -200.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>One last note:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>It has been pointed out by several people that one of the most striking things about the whole Enochian system is that How Much You Put Into It seems to matter a great deal more than How You Do It. Perhaps there's something to James' reasoning, after all.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>I make no claims that this guide will make you any better (though hopefully no worse) of a magician. My intent was solely to attempt to compare the various systems of pronunciation with Dr. Dee's original notes on the subject. It is to him, and to my many mentors both within and without the Order, that this paper is dedicated with respect and love.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Love is the law, love under will.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Christeos Pir\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Fr. PLAPLI IADNAMAD. pla-pli Iad-na-mad.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>PART TWO\u003C/p>\u003Cp>As noted before, there is a lot of room for debate in those areas where Dee's notes are inconclusive or contradictory. This guide is my own interpretation (and occasionally, I admit, extrapolation) of Dee's pronunciation. Another, that of Donald Laycock, is in the Appendix to this paper. It is my opinion that often the differences between these variations are more of emphasis rather than any fundamental discord: between \"ee\" and \"ih\" for instance.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>In the version of the Calls that follows, the first line is that of the Calls as printed in Causabon (except where noted), the second and third are my phonetic version with possible variations.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>PRONUNCIATION KEY\u003C/p>\u003Cp>A - as in \"father\", except \"ag\" rhymes with \"nag\" C - as k before a, o, u (some exceptions) - as s before i, e (some exceptions) E - stressed: as in \"obey\" - unstressed: as in \"beg\" (some exceptions) G - as (hard) g before a, o, u - usually as j before i, e, and word-final, occasionally in other places, some exceptions H - usually as h, except in combinations (ch, ph, sh, th); either silent or slightly aspirated in certain locations (eg:\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Comselh). This last does not apply to the phonetic notation that follows: duh-loo-gah (dluga) does not end with an aspi- rated sound. I - stressed: as in \"machine\" - unstressed: as in \"pill\" I,J - as y or jh (\"ge\" as in \"garage\") in initial position and before a vowel O - stressed: as in \"go\" - unstressed: as in \"go\" or as in \"pot\", especially in -och-\u003C/p>\u003Cp>(rhymes with \"botch\") Q,QU - as \"kuh\", or \"kwuh\" (as in \"quick\") U - usually as in \"rule\", but occasionally as in \"mug\" - in initial position may be pronounced as \"yew\" X - as in \"fox\" Y - usually as under key for I and I,J Z - as in \"zoo\"; in a very few words, as zod CH - as k in most positions, but -\"och\" rhymes with \"botch\" PH - as f SH - as sh TH - as th\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Double vowels should be pronounced only partly separately, with an elided pause between them: QAA becomes Kuh-ah-ah, with only the barest hesitation. The First Call\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Ol sonf vorsg, goho iad balt, lansh calz vonpho: sobra zol ror I Ol sonf vors-juh, goho yad balt, lonsh calz vonfo: sobra zol ror ee vors-guh zod-ol\u003C/p>\u003Cp>ta nazpsad graa ta malprg: ds holq qaa ta naz-puh-sad grah-ah ta malpurj: des hol-kwuh-uh kuh-ah-ah hol-kuh-uh kwuh-ah-ah\u003C/p>\u003Cp>nothoa zimz, od commah ta nobloh zien: soba thil gnonp no-tho-ah zeemz, od com-mah ta no-blo zee-en: soba theel guh-non-puh zimz thil guh-nomp\u003C/p>\u003Cp>prge aldi, ds urbs oboleh grsam. Nonsa go-hoo-lim, meek-ma fee-seez Jha-ee-dah! mik-ma\u003C/p>\u003Cp>adoian mad, Iaod bliorb, sabaooaona chis a-do-ee-an mad, Ee-ah-od blee-orb, sa-ba-oh-oh-ah-oh-na kis\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Yah-od\u003C/p>\u003Cp>luciftias peripsol, ds abraassa noncf netaaib loo-keef-tee-as pay-reep-sol, des a-bra-as-sa non-suf ne-tah-ah-eeb loo-seef-tee-as peh-reep-sol\u003C/p>\u003Cp>caosgi od tilb adphaht damploz, tooat noncf ka-os-jee od teelb ad-fa-tuh dam-ploz, to-oh-at non-suf ka-os-gee\u003C/p>\u003Cp>gmicalzoma l rasd tofglo marb yarry idoigo od guh-mee-kal-zo-ma el-ras-duh tof-glo marb yar-ree eed-wee-go od ee-doo-ee-go\u003C/p>\u003Cp>torzulp iaodaf, gohol; caosga tabaord saanir tor-zoolp ya-oh-daf, go-hol; ka-os-ga ta-ba-ord sa-ah-neer\u003C/p>\u003Cp>od christeos yrpoil tiobl, busdir tilb noaln od kris-tay-os eer-po-eel tee-oh-bul, boos-deer teel-buh no-al-nuh yur-po-eel til-buh\u003C/p>\u003Cp>paid orsba od dodrmni zylna. It will be noted that hyphens are frequently inserted in words. These only emphasise the need to give full value to a vowel, or to a syllable, and do not call for a real break in the utterance.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>DONALD LAYCOCK'S PRONUNCIATION KEY\u003C/p>\u003Cp>(from \"The Complete Enochian Dictionary\" pps 46-47.)\u003C/p>\u003Cp>A - long (stressed), as in \"lah-di-dah\" - short (unstressed), as in French \"patte\" B - usually as English b, but silent between m and another consonant, or after m finally C - as k before a, o, u (with some exceptions) - as s before i, e (with many exceptions), and in clusters of consonants (noncf = nonsf) CH - as k in most positions, but as ch finally D - as d in all positions E - when stressed, as in French \"fee\" - when unstressed, as in \"bed\" F - as f in all positions G - as (hard) g before a, o, u - as j before i, e, in final position, after d, and in clusters of consonants H - as h in most positions (except in combinations ch, ph, sh, th); silent after a vowel, but the vowel is lengthened I - when stressed, as in (French) \"machine\" - when unstressed, as in \"bit\" - in combinations: ai as in \"fly\"; ei as in \"eight\"; oi as in\u003C/p>\u003Cp>\"boil\" - as y in word-initial position before a vowel (Iad = Yad) K - as k in all positions L - as l in all positions M - as m in all positions N - as n in all positions O - when stressed, as in French \"mot\" - when unstressed, as in \"not\" - in combinations: oi as in \"boil\"; ou as in \"bout\"; oo as in\u003C/p>\u003Cp>\"fool\" P - as p except in combination ph PH - as f Q,QU - as kw (qu in \"quick\") -- but the word q is pronounced kwa R - as r in \"right\" (but may be rolled) S - usually as s in \"sit\" - sometimes z, in places where this is more natural in English\u003C/p>\u003Cp>(lrasd = elrazd) SH - as sh in \"ship\" T - as t except in combination th TH - as th in \"thank\" U - as oo in \"boot\", or u in \"put\" - in initial position as \"yew\" - as v or w before another vowel, and in word-final position X - as x in \"fox\" Y - as y in word-initial position before a vowel - as the letter I before a consonant, and in word-final position Z - as z in \"zoo\"; in a very few words, as zod\u003C/p>\u003Cp>BIBLIOGRAPHY\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Causabon, Meric., D.D., A True and Faithful Relation of What Passed for Many Years Between Dr. John Dee and some Spirits, Magickal Childe, 1992, New York, NY.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Denning, Melita, and Phillips, Osborne, Mysteria Magica, Llewellyn Publications, 1992, St. Paul, MN.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>James, Geoffrey, The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee, Heptangle Books, 1989, Gillette, NJ.\u003C/p>\u003Cp>Laycock, Donald C., The Complete Enochian Dictionary, Askin Publishers, 1978, London, England, UK.\u001a\u003C/p>",wordCount:9714,footnotes:[]},bookTextModules:{snapshot:{page:"book-text",publishedAt:"2026-09-21T07:03:20.828Z",layoutVariant:"desktop",frame:{template:"main-right",config:{base:{slots:{"right-rail":{}}}}},sections:[{id:"main",label:"Book text",root:{kind:"zone",id:"main",order:0,width:{},config:{base:{direction:"column",gap:24}},children:[{kind:"placement",id:"ads-layout:book-text:desktop:before",order:0,moduleId:"adsense-slot",width:{base:"full"},hidden:{},config:{label:"Advertisement",sizes:"responsive",format:"display",sticky:false,unitId:"1169221565",position:"before",minPageHeight:0}},{kind:"placement",id:"ads-layout:book-text:desktop:usb",order:1,moduleId:"usb-ad",width:{base:"full"},hidden:{},config:{body:"Every text in the library on a single USB drive. Enochian","@type":"Book",author:{name:"Christeos Pir","@type":"Person"},inLanguage:"en"},inLanguage:"en",description:"AN ESSAY ON ENOCHIAN PRONUNCIATION by Christeos Pir Fr. VITRIOL 335 0o O.T.O. Do what thou wilt shall be the whole of the Law. This paper is divided into two sections: a comparison of some systems of pronunciation of the Enochian or Angelic language as used in the nineteen Calls of the tablets, and an attempt to write the Calls in such a manner that they may be pronounced according to Dr. Dee's notes. Part One: The first section compares three versions of the Calls: that of the Aurum Solis as published in the Llewellyn edition of Denning and Phillips' \"Mysteria Magica\"; Geoffrey James' \"corrected\" calls as published in the Heptangle edition of \"The Enochian Evocation of Dr. John Dee\"; and Dr. Dee's original notes as published in the Magickal Childe edition of Meric Causabon's \"True and Faithful Relation...\". I have compared these systems word-by-word, with the Aurum Solis and James versions on the first two lines, and Dee's on the third. Example: Aurum Solis: OL^ SONuF VORoSiG, GOHO I-AD BALaT, Geoffrey James: OL SONF VORSG, GOHO IAD BALT, Dee: Ol sonf vorsg, goho yad balt, Where Dee has separated a word into its syllables (eg, Im ua mar), I have hyphenated it so as to more easily discern between word divisions and syllable divisions (Im-ua-mar). Where he has shown stress by using an accent, I have used boldface (q-a-an). In a number of places his marginalia give more than one version of a word, usually in order to clarify its pronunciation; other times he has occasionally written an example for the same purpose -- these I have placed in footnotes. There are also one or two places where he has offered variant spellings: these, too, I have placed in the footnotes. I have attempted to standardize the punctuation, arbitrarily picking one of several possible systems and applying it to all of them in a manner somewhat reminiscent of Procrustes. One problem that had to be dealt with in re-typing the Aurum Solis version is their system of transliteration, which uses several symbols outside of the English alphabet. I have made two changes to their system, solely in order to use symbols that my computer could produce, as follows (the revised key, which is adapted from \"Mysteria Magica\", and applies only to the AS version, is given in the Appendix): ^ represents the neutral vowel, as the first vowel sound in \"parade,\" or the second vowel sound in \"column.\" # represents the guttural, pronounced as the \"ch\" in German (Woche, suchen). It is to be noted that James' version contains a number of differences from Dee's originals. These, as I understand it, are the result of his research into, and attempts to rectify, variations among the Calls themselves, and between the Enochian of the Calls and their English translations. I have not done any research in this area, and leave it up to the reader to decide which version to follow. Some of those corrections which are meant to fix transcription errors on Dee's part directly contradict some of Dee's own pronunciation",mainEntityOfPage:"https://www.sacred-texts.com/book/an-essay-on-the-pronunciation-of-enochian/reader/pr-calls"}],"@context":"https://schema.org"},targetKeyword:"Enochian Calls pronunciation",secondaryKeywords:["Dr. Dee pronunciation","Aurum Solis Calls","Geoffrey James Enochian"],ogImageWidth:null,ogImageHeight:null,ogImageAlt:null,ogLocale:null,twitterImageAlt:null,twitterSite:null,inLanguage:"en",hreflangVersions:[],indexability:"index",noindexReason:null,imageAlt:{},entitySameAs:[],entityType:"webpage",dataSourceTier:1,templateId:null,templateVersion:null,evidenceRefs:[],publishedAt:new Date(1787664824572),publishedByUserId:null,sourceHash:"chapter:5f42dfd27c5224e3cba538b73fb33901"},chapterLinks:[{id:309143,chapterNumber:309143,chapterTitle:"pr calls",chapterSlug:"pr-calls",href:"/pag/pr_calls.txt"}]},uses:{search_params:["chapter"],params:["bookSlug","chapterSlug","slug","categorySlug","topicSlug","id","bookId","pageId","orderId","productId","runId"],route:1,url:1}}}({}))],
